# Owner decision packet — the variant tier (1,127 rows, 1,126 rebuilt ids)

**This is a question, not an approval.** The agent did not approve, reject or defer anything in this packet. Every variant row stays out of every serving tier.

## 1. What is being decided

The Phase-3 rebuild could not reconstruct these spans **exactly** from the authoritative word-timed artifact: the recorded corpus text came from the markdown display transcript while the timings came from the voted word-timed artifact, and for these spans the two disagree in wording. They are therefore *variants*: the text is a near-match, not the span.

## 2. The counts, stated without a hidden duplicate

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---:|
| Phase-3 variant source rows | **1127** |
| Distinct rebuilt ids in the Phase-3 variant set | **1126** |
| Variant tier rows after the collision repair (one row per source span) | **1125** |
| Deterministic-id collision quarantine rows | **2** |
| Colliding rebuilt ids | **1** |

> The earlier report called the difference a *byte-identical duplicate*. That was **wrong**. It is not a duplicate row: it is a **deterministic-id collision** — two different live spans that produced the same rebuilt id. Both are now quarantined and neither is silently kept.

## 3. The collision record

### Collided rebuilt id `b7d1c4a06dccace5e689ab7a18d7f0668b4fadebb972bdab62f0ccb34063c6d9`

- Namespace: `rebuilt_corpus_v2`
- Members: **2** (distinct source spans: 2)
- Cause: **`incomplete_deterministic_id_namespace`** — the rebuilt id is a function of the reconstructed word window only, so two distinct source spans that resolve to the same window produce the same id
- Resolution status: **unresolved**
- Namespace fix applied: **False**
- Why no namespace fix: adding the source span identity to the id namespace re-keys every span in the corpus, which invalidates the 1,600 Phase-2 verdicts (keyed by the current rebuilt ids), the rights join, the tier manifests and the pinned local-review contract. That is not backward-compatible, so no namespace change was made and the collision stays unresolved.
- Rebuilt id namespace: `['guru_id', 'video_id', 'corpus_version', 'word_start', 'word_end', 'transcript_hash']`
- Missing from the namespace: `source_span_identity (the live v10 source_span_id)`

| live source span id | video | source row | rebuilt window | live window |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| `3c4862e8d4c36aee1211215171a662516bbedb20b2120dc8326a818deb1a13a8` | `MmiI3xo5Ju4` | 729 | 2284–2301 | 2284–2307 |
| `8a330f28575000c1e3de668e0819655de21f58bca6b0fd9fdcf34afed34630d5` | `MmiI3xo5Ju4` | 985 | 2284–2301 | 2263–2284 |

- Evidence: 1 distinct rebuilt window(s), ['fp-norm-unicode-v1'] normalization version(s), ['23e384a01051942fefb2a9196f6140cfaeac1d3ccdfe2f7994149c122a190bb5'] ASR artifact hash(es) — so the cause is **not** normalization and **not** an input artifact conflict.

## 4. The three choices

**(a) Rebuild the source text and timing from a single voted layer.** Fix the upstream disagreement (use the voted word-timed artifact for BOTH the text and the offsets) and re-run Phase 3. This produces exact spans, but it re-keys the rebuilt ids, which invalidates the Phase-2 verdicts, the rights join and the tier manifests — all of it must be re-run. It is the only option that removes the variants rather than dispositioning them.

**(b) Retain them as non-serving review variants.** Keep the rows as they are, permanently outside every serving tier, as review material for a future rebuild.

**(c) Exclude them permanently.** Drop the variant population from the corpus altogether and record the exclusion.

## Owner decision record

`choice` is one of `rebuild_from_one_voted_transcript_layer`, `retain_review_only_never_serve` or `exclude_permanently`. `deterministic_id_collision` stays `remain_excluded`: a variant decision never admits the two collision members.

This block is a **copy** of the owner action form's block for this topic (`audits/evidence/FIRST_PERSON_OWNER_DECISIONS_2026-10-22.md`). The form is the source of truth; the release-gate controller fails closed if the two disagree. `approved` accepts `"pending"`, `"true"` or `"false"` — there is no default approval.

  "decision_id": "b7_variant_tier",

  "deterministic_id_collision": "remain_excluded"
